# Catalog Review: Resources for Training, Improving and Testing the BCF Coding Agent

**Prepared:** 2026-09-30
**Input:** `coderprog_catalog.json` (3,130 records: 2,719 books, 407 courses, 1 template, 3 site pages)
**Goal:** identify every catalog item that would help train, improve or test the BCF agent for a winning Gemma 4
Developer Agent submission (and the paper-track Writeup), then rank them.

**Result:** **88 useful resources** (61 books, 27 courses):
9 scored 80-100, 21 scored 65-79, and 58 scored 50-64.
Everything else scored below 50 or was judged not useful.

---

## 1. Method

| Step | What was done                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Records        |
| ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------: |
| 1    | Loaded the full catalog; removed 3 site pages (Contact, DMCA, Privacy Policy) with no content                                                                                                                                                   | 3,127          |
| 2    | Keyword-theme screen over **title + synopsis** of every record (18 themes: agents, AI coding, fine-tuning/RL, evals, serving, RAG/graphs, testing, debugging, program analysis, Python, statistics, research writing, infrastructure, and more) | 3,127 scored   |
| 3    | **Manual read of every title** (all 3,127, sorted by screen score) to catch items the keywords missed                                                                                                                                           | 3,127          |
| 4    | Targeted synopsis search for high-signal terms (Google ADK, vLLM, LoRA/QLoRA, GRPO/DPO/RLVR, pytest, debugging, information theory, BM25, evals, SWE-bench)                                                                                     | 17 term groups |
| 5    | Synopsis review of the 131 shortlisted candidates; scoring against the rubric below                                                                                                                                                             | 131            |
| 6    | Kept items scoring at least 50; tables generated by script directly from the JSON so metadata is verbatim                                                                                                                                       | 88             |

No catalog item mentions SWE-bench, and none covers Gemma 4 post-training specifically. Six items mention Google ADK;
only two cover it substantively: #17 (ADK code examples) and #35 (a full ADK week).

### 1.1 Scoring rubric (0-100)

| Criterion                               | Weight | What earns points                                                                                                                                           |
| --------------------------------------- | -----: | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Relevance to a lever in the master plan | 40     | Directly supports a planned workstream: LoRA/RFT/DPO training, harness/skills/gates, evaluation factory, throughput budget, whitepaper math, graph analyses |
| Actionability within the 6-week window  | 25     | Hands-on, code-level, immediately applicable; not strategy or leadership material                                                                           |
| Depth, authority and currency           | 20     | Recognized author or publisher; 2025-2026 content; focused rather than survey                                                                               |
| Fit to competition constraints          | 15     | Offline, open-weights, single local model, Python, declarative ADK, vLLM; penalized for hosted-API, TypeScript or cloud-only stacks                         |

Score bands: **80-100** use now (core); **65-79** high value for a specific workstream; **50-64** situational or
alternative format. Tags in the Justification column: [Train] post-training, [Serve] inference/throughput,
[Harness] scaffold/skills/gates, [Eval] evaluation, [Spec] spec-first/testing discipline, [Paper] whitepaper,
[Graph] code graphs/retrieval, [Python] domain skills, [Infra] environment. Plan references (G1-G6, E1-E7, J1-J16,
X1-X12, DG1-DG6, L1) point to the master plan and the BCF review documents from earlier today.

### 1.2 Metadata notes

- **Pub. year `n/a*`:** the catalog's `publication_year` for video courses is a parser artifact. The extractor read
  the video resolution (for example "1920×1080") as the year, or left it empty. Course post dates are real.
- **Length:** books show pages; courses show lessons and runtime parsed from `raw_meta_line` (courses have no ISBN
  or page count in the catalog).
- **All remaining fields** (`category_slug`, `post_date_raw`, `raw_meta_line`, `extraction_status`,
  `missing_fields`, `extracted_at`, `homepage_url`, `image_url`, `local_image_path`) are in **Appendix A**, in the
  same rank order. Pipe characters inside field values (for example in `raw_meta_line`) are shown as "·" so the
  tables render in every Markdown viewer. A machine-readable copy of the full selected records is in
  `input/kagglecomp/scratch/claude_work/catalog/selected_resources.json`.

### 1.3 Important usage note

These resources are for **your learning and design decisions**, not for training data. Do not feed book or course
text into LoRA training sets or into the submission. The competition requires external data to be publicly available
and license-compatible, and winners must release their training pipeline under an open-source license. Use legitimately
licensed copies (publisher accounts, subscriptions or libraries) and cite the published editions in the paper.

---

| Ultimate LLMOps with Langfuse; LLM Observability with Langfuse (2124, 1361)                                | Langfuse is parked in the plan (heavy self-hosting, data-security risk); JSONL + DuckDB suffices.              |

| Local LLMs via Ollama & LM Studio; Ollama in Action (1895, 2752)                                           | The competition serves Gemma with vLLM; Ollama/LM Studio skills don't transfer to scoring.                     |
| NVIDIA GPU Infrastructure Fundamentals; Designing NVIDIA AI Infrastructure (3055, 2680)                    | Data-center operations; not needed for one RTX 5090 plus Kaggle's fixed 4x L4.                                 |
| Chaos Engineering for AI Infrastructure (2644)                                                             | Infrastructure-level fault injection, not code-level faults in Python repositories.                            |
| AI-Driven Software Testing (126); Full Stack Testing (2382)                                                | Enterprise QA process; little on repository-level bug repair.                                                  |
| Graph Neural Networks; Graph Representation Learning; Guide to Graph Algorithms (2995, 2659, 1087)         | Training models on graphs; the competition graph and tools are fixed. Covered well enough by #50 for analysis. |
| Bayesian Statistical Methods; Foundations of Bayesian Statistics (365, 957)                                | Solid but redundant with the information-theory/Bayes and workflow picks already listed.                       |
| About 40 Claude Code / Cursor / Copilot / vibe-coding app-building courses (e.g. 508, 522, 2174, 655, 116) | Tool-usage tutorials for building web apps; a few representatives are kept for design ideas.                   |
| LangChain, LangGraph, CrewAI, n8n, Bedrock, Azure Foundry agent courses (e.g. 1745, 81, 92, 344, 1534)     | Frameworks that cannot be used in a declarative ADK submission.                                                |
| MCP books and courses (e.g. 1555, 2884, 753, 82)                                                           | MCP servers are not available inside the offline, harness-controlled sandbox.                                  |
| RAG and vector-database books (e.g. 1827, 1828, 2128, 2169, 2171)                                          | Vector-DB infrastructure is irrelevant; embeddings and graph tools are provided by the harness.                |
| AI security and red-teaming titles (e.g. 2824, 2564, 1857, 2306)                                           | Not related to issue-resolution performance.                                                                   |
| FastAPI courses and 'Prompting FastAPI' (933, 2863)                                                        | Familiarity with a training repo only; the hidden test set uses private repositories.                          |
| Practical Debugging for Embedded ARM Systems (1698)                                                        | Debugging, but for embedded C/ARM, not Python repositories.                                                    |
| AI Cost Playbook (2886)                                                                                    | API token FinOps; the competition cost is wall-clock on fixed hardware.                                        |
| Generic RL textbooks (1862, 1863, 2596)                                                                    | Classic RL; the LLM-specific post-training titles are more direct.                                             |

# Catalog Review (0dayprizrak): Resources for Training, Improving and Testing the BCF Coding Agent

**Prepared:** 2026-09-30
**Input:** `0dayprizrak_catalog.json`: 50,848 records (50,847 scraped OK, 1 scrape error), mostly untyped ebooks,
PDFs, EPUBs and MP4 courses spanning every subject (fiction, cooking, fitness, engineering, IT)
**Companion:** the earlier review of `coderprog_catalog.json` in `2026-09-30_Catalog/`. Items that duplicate a title
already ranked there are flagged in the column **Also in coderprog review**.

**Result:** **103 useful resources** (52 video courses, 51 books/other):
6 scored 80-100, 22 scored 65-79, 75 scored 50-64.
**18 duplicate a title in the coderprog review; 85 are new.**

---

## 1. Method

| Step | What was done                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Records / families   |
| ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------: |
| 1    | Loaded all records; dropped the one scrape error                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | 50,847               |
| 2    | Removed exact duplicate titles                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | 47,448 unique titles |
| 3    | Grouped near-duplicates into title families (Udemy update variants, mirrors, video vs. book editions)                                                                                                                                                                             | 45,428 families      |
| 4    | Scored **every** record's title + synopsis with the same 18-theme keyword screen used for the coderprog review, plus 9 high-precision boosters (SWE-bench, ADK, vLLM, GRPO/RLVR, QLoRA/PEFT/Unsloth, agent harness, Gemma, fault localization/program repair, information theory) | 45,428 scored        |
| 5    | **Manually read every family title with screen score above 10**                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 3,221 titles read    |
| 6    | Targeted synopsis search across all records for 12 high-signal term groups (Gemma, ADK, vLLM, GRPO/DPO, LoRA/PEFT, program repair/fault localization, pytest, information theory, harness, Kaggle, BM25/AST/static analysis, evals)                                               | 12 term groups       |
| 7    | **Recall check:** random sample of 500 families with score 10 or below, titles read manually                                                                                                                                                                                      | 0 relevant of 500    |
| 8    | Synopsis review of 137 shortlisted families; scored against the rubric                                                                                                                                                                                                            | 137                  |
| 9    | Kept items scoring at least 50; tables generated by script from the JSON so metadata is verbatim                                                                                                                                                                                  | 103                  |

**Recall estimate.** 0 relevant items in a random sample of 500 low-score families gives a 95% upper bound of about
0.6% (rule of three). Applied to the 42,154 low-score families, that is at most about 250 missed items that would be
even marginally relevant, and in practice far fewer, because the targeted term searches in step 6 also cover the
low-score pool. No catalog item mentions SWE-bench.

### 1.1 Scoring rubric (0-100), identical to the coderprog review

| Criterion                               | Weight | What earns points                                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------- | -----: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Relevance to a lever in the master plan | 40     | Directly supports LoRA/RFT/DPO training, harness/skills/gates, evaluation factory, throughput budget, whitepaper math, graph analyses |
| Actionability within the 6-week window  | 25     | Hands-on, code-level, immediately applicable                                                                                          |
| Depth, authority and currency           | 20     | Recognized author, publisher or platform; 2025-2026 content; focused                                                                  |
| Fit to competition constraints          | 15     | Offline, open-weights, single local model, Python, declarative ADK, vLLM                                                              |

Tags in the Justification column: [Train], [Serve], [Harness], [Eval], [Spec], [Paper], [Graph], [Python], [Infra].
Plan references (G, E, J, X, DG, L, FM, T) point to the master plan and the BCF review documents from earlier today.

### 1.2 Metadata notes

- Most records have no genre, language, year or author (the source posts rarely include them); these show as `n/a`.
  Where an ISBN field held `N/A`, it is shown as `n/a`.
- **Download links are intentionally omitted.** The catalog stores file-hosting mirror URLs (Rapidgator, DDownload,
  AlfaFile and similar). Appendix A lists only the link count and provider names. Acquire titles through legitimate
  channels (publisher sites, subscriptions such as O'Reilly/Manning/Pluralsight/LinkedIn Learning/Udemy, or libraries),
  using the title, author and ISBN given here.
- Pipe characters inside field values are shown as "·" so the tables render in every Markdown viewer.
- The full selected records (all fields except download URLs, plus family member IDs) are in
  `input/kagglecomp/scratch/claude_work/prizrak/selected_resources.json`.

### 1.3 Usage note (same as the coderprog review)

Use these resources for learning and design decisions, not as training data. Do not put book or course text into LoRA
training sets or the submission. Competition rules require public, license-compatible external data, and winners must
open-source their pipeline. Cite published editions in the paper.

---

## Appendix B - Near-Misses Considered and Excluded

| Title(s) and record index                                                                                                                                                                                         | Why excluded                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Self-published, mass-produced AI ebooks (e.g. Vishal Uttam Mane RLHF/fine-tuning titles 44176, 39963, 11432, 44174; Ajit Singh titles such as 7464, 3638; 40338; Peter E. Poisson ADK titles 41937, 43797, 42615) | Thin or unverifiable depth; stronger ranked alternatives cover the same topics. One Poisson ADK ebook is kept at score 50 as a fallback.                  |
| Practical Reinforcement Learning for ML Engineers (17976)                                                                                                                                                         | Course language is Arabic per the catalog metadata.                                                                                                       |
| Dozens of LangChain, LangGraph, CrewAI, AutoGen, n8n, Copilot Studio, Bedrock and Azure Foundry agent courses                                                                                                     | Frameworks that cannot be used in a declarative ADK submission.                                                                                           |
| About 150 Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot, Codex and vibe-coding app-building course families                                                                                                                        | Tool-usage tutorials for building apps; representatives with transferable design ideas are ranked.                                                        |
| MCP, A2A and agent-protocol courses and books                                                                                                                                                                     | MCP servers and A2A are unavailable in the offline harness sandbox.                                                                                       |
| AI security, prompt injection, red-teaming, OWASP LLM courses                                                                                                                                                     | Not related to issue-resolution performance.                                                                                                              |
| LLM token/cost optimization courses (9280, 47454, 47704)                                                                                                                                                          | API-cost oriented; the competition cost is wall-clock on fixed hardware. The context-optimization course (#51) is kept.                                   |
| RAG and vector-database courses and books                                                                                                                                                                         | Vector infrastructure is irrelevant; the harness provides embeddings and graph tools.                                                                     |
| Root-cause-analysis and troubleshooting titles for industrial, electrical, network or IT domains (e.g. 17844, 50118, 9563, 11723)                                                                                 | Different domain; the software-specific debugging items are ranked.                                                                                       |
| Top 100 Interesting Bugs (19962)                                                                                                                                                                                  | Security bug-bounty content, not functional software faults.                                                                                              |
| Generic deep-RL books and courses (e.g. 45818, 16248, 22307)                                                                                                                                                      | Classic RL; the LLM-specific post-training items are more direct.                                                                                         |
| Kimi K3, GPT-OSS, DeepSeek-usage and other model-specific courses                                                                                                                                                 | Different model families; only Gemma-specific items are kept.                                                                                             |
| TypeSafe AI Jev Essentials (50820)                                                                                                                                                                                | Hosted model that cannot run in the sandbox; data-use risk for dev-time use.                                                                              |
| Exact and near-duplicate records                                                                                                                                                                                  | 3,399 exact duplicates and further near-duplicates (Udemy update variants, mirrors) are collapsed into families; family members are listed in Appendix A. |

# FOSS Tools Review: Resources for Training, Improving and Testing the BCF Coding Agent

*Compiled 2026-09-30 from `foss_tools/` (githubdigest.json and the 16 grokmuse reports). Third review in the series,
after `2026-09-30_Catalog/` (coderprog) and `2026-09-30_Catalog_0dayprizrak/`; same rubric, so scores are comparable.*

**Bottom line.** 58 of about 4,200 distinct repositories are useful for the BCF agent (1.4%). Most of the
collection is tooling for human-driven coding agents (skills packs, memory layers, MCP servers, orchestration UIs),
which does not carry over to a declarative, offline, fixed-tool harness. The useful part is concentrated and strong. It
contains a public source of SWE training tasks for repos outside the four training repos, a complete LoRA training
stack for one RTX 5090, and an MIT-licensed open-weights coding teacher post-trained on Gemma 4. It also has
text-optimization tools for the part of the submission that is text.

---

## 1. Method

| Step | What was done                                                                                                                                                            | Items         |
| ---: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------- |
| 1    | Loaded `githubdigest.json`: repo records with synopses; 3,351 carry README text and 2,300 carry GitHub API metadata                                                      | 4,095         |
| 2    | Loaded the 16 grokmuse reports and extracted every GitHub link                                                                                                           | 352 (129 new) |
| 3    | Weighted keyword screen (13 term groups: SWE-bench/repair, coding agents, fine-tuning/RL, Gemma, ADK, vLLM, code graphs, context, eval, testing, prompts, diffs, Python) | 4,095 scored  |
| 4    | Hand review of one-line digests for every record scoring 25+ or mentioning SWE-bench, Gemma or ADK                                                                       | 613           |
| 5    | Targeted name search across all records (trainers, RL stacks, SWE datasets, prompt optimizers, tracing, linters)                                                         | 195 hits      |
| 6    | Full read of the LoRA/fine-tuning grokmuse report (35 repos plus its not-kept list); agentic and software lanes of the other 15 reports; all 129 non-digest repos        | 16 reports    |
| 7    | README evidence check for the finalists: claims, licenses, Gemma 4 support, benchmark numbers, release status                                                            | ~60           |
| 8    | Scored against the rubric; kept items scoring 42 or more; tables generated by script so metadata is verbatim                                                             | 58            |

**Recall check.** A random sample of 250 records from the 3,482-record low-score pool contained none that would score
42 or more (the closest were `guidance-ai/guidance`, `astral-sh/uv` and `aws-samples/sample-specship`). The 95% upper
bound is about 1.2% of the pool, so at most about 40 missed items, and in practice far fewer, because step 5 searched
the low-score pool by name.

### 1.1 Scoring rubric (0-100), identical to the earlier reviews

| Criterion                               | Weight | What earns points                                                                                                   |
| --------------------------------------- | -----: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Relevance to a lever in the master plan | 40     | Directly supports LoRA/RFT/DPO training, the task-mining data factory, harness and skills, evaluation, or the paper |
| Actionability before 2026-12-02         | 25     | Usable now on the MAIN box (one RTX 5090, 32 GB) and AUX sandbox workers, without rented clusters                   |
| Depth, authority and currency           | 20     | Recognized lab or maintainer, peer-reviewed or measured results, active in 2026                                     |
| Fit to competition constraints          | 15     | Offline sandbox, single base model, LoRA of rank 128 or less under 3 GiB, declarative ADK, vLLM, OSI licensing      |

The `R·A·D·F` column shows the four sub-scores. Tags in the Justification column: [Data], [Train], [Serve],
[Harness], [Prompt], [Eval], [Graph], [Paper], [Infra]. Plan references (L5, L6, L9, L10, M4, DG4, Path A/B) point to
`2026-09-30/01_Roadmap_and_Master_Checklist.md`; "compendium finding N" points to `bcf/BCF-Reference-Compendium.md`
section 0.3.

### 1.2 Metadata notes

- **Two sources, two date meanings.** Digest records show the date the repo was seen in a video, post or link list
  (`seen`). Grokmuse-only records show the last push date printed in the report (`push`).
- `n/r` means not recorded in the collection. Many digest records (for example vLLM, OpenHands, DSPy) have no GitHub
  API block, and the grokmuse report gives no license fields. Check the license on GitHub before reusing code.
- Digest descriptions are the GitHub description, or the collection's synopsis when that is missing, cut at a sentence
  boundary. Grokmuse descriptions are compressed from the report.
- Merged rows: `unslothai/unsloth` includes the digest record `unslothai/unsloth-zoo`; `hiyouga/LlamaFactory` includes
  the digest record `hiyouga/LLaMA-Factory`; `OpenHands/OpenHands` includes two digest records.
- The remaining metadata (homepage, topics, open issues, archived flag, video or post, timestamp, generated keywords)
  is in Appendix A. Thumbnails and README text are omitted.
- Pipe characters inside field values are shown as "·" so the tables render in every Markdown viewer.

### 1.3 License and usage note

Almost nothing here ships inside `submission.zip`. The submission holds YAML, prompts, skills and LoRA weights. Most of
these tools are used to build those, so their licenses matter mainly for the open-source release winners must publish
(OSI license with no limit on commercial use). Two points need care. **Training data and teacher outputs** end up in
the adapter weights, so prefer MIT/Apache sources and record provenance. **NOASSERTION, GPL or leaked text**
(augment-swebench-agent, context-mode, awesome-system-prompts, swe-rl) is for ideas only; do not paste it into prompts.

---

## 2. Key Findings (what changes the plan)

| #   | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Action                                                                                                                                                                                |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | **An open, MIT-licensed SWE teacher exists.** Ornith-1 (post-trained on Gemma 4 and Qwen 3.5) reports 69.4 on SWE-bench Verified for its 9B, against 52.0 for Gemma4-31B in the same table. The plan ruled out frontier-API distillation for ToS reasons and fell back to self-distillation. The Gemma-based 31B-Dense is announced but not linked.                                                | Sprint 1: serve Ornith-1.0-9B on the RTX 5090 inside the swegemma harness and measure its resolve rate on DEV. If it clearly beats Gemma 4, add a teacher arm to RFT data generation. |
| 2   | **The 129 training tasks cover four repos; the hidden set is ~120 private-repo tasks.** SWE-smith (any repo to tasks), SWE-Gym (real Python issues), R2E-Gym (commit-derived environments), SWE-bench-Live and SWE-rebench (fresh, decontaminated) together cover the M4 data factory.                                                                                                             | Build M4 from SWE-smith first (fastest to scale); build PSEUDO-PRIVATE from SWE-bench-Live/SWE-rebench tasks created after the Gemma 4 cutoff.                                        |
| 3   | **Trainer choice for one 32 GB GPU.** Unsloth is the most memory-efficient path for Path B (QLoRA on bf16). Axolotl is the only trainer whose README in this collection explicitly lists Gemma 4. TRL sits underneath both. Liger's fused cross-entropy targets Gemma's large-vocabulary logits cost. The RL stacks (verl, SkyRL, rLLM, OpenRLHF, NeMo Gym) assume multiple GPUs for a 31B policy. | Unsloth primary, axolotl as a cross-check, llm-compressor for Path A. Defer RL until DG4 passes and only with rented GPUs.                                                            |
| 4   | **Text is a trainable surface.** SkillOpt optimizes skill text for a frozen agent under held-out gating. JetBrains measured that efficiency rules helped when injected into the prompt and did nothing as an optional skill folder. FrontierHarness Eval shows a 16.7-point pass-rate spread (50.0% to 66.7%) from harness changes alone, with the model fixed.                                    | Put budget-critical rules in `instruction`, not `skills/`. Run SkillOpt (or DSPy/GEPA) on phase prompts with a validation split disjoint from DEV.                                    |
| 5   | **Context compaction splits trajectories.** The harness compacts context at 14,336 tokens. Dressage's handling of compaction-split trajectories applies directly to RFT data: a long successful run is not one clean training sequence.                                                                                                                                                            | Build SFT samples from the post-compaction context the model actually saw; check loss masking per segment (DG4 diagnosis).                                                            |
| 6   | **Paper evidence.** Agentless (fixed phases), AutoCodeRover (AST search plus spectrum-based fault localization), SWE-Swiss (multi-task localization and repair), Graft (code map: 66% vs 54%), FrontierHarness Eval (harness matters) and oh-my-pi (edit format: 6.7% to 68.3%) all support BCF's claims. Caution: `interference-search` is not about fault interference.                          | Cite the peer-reviewed ones (Agentless, AutoCodeRover, SWE-Swiss, SWE-agent) in the related-work section. Label repo-reported numbers as claims.                                      |

---

## 3. Start Here (top picks per workstream)

| Workstream                        | Pick first                          | Then                                               | Why this order                                                                                 |
| --------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Data factory (M4, PSEUDO-PRIVATE) | SWE-smith                           | SWE-Gym, SWE-bench-Live, R2E-Gym, SWE-rebench      | Scales to any Python repo fastest; fresh tasks guard the held-out estimate                     |
| Trajectory generation (L6 RFT)    | vLLM (exact scoring flags)          | Ornith-1 as teacher, rLLM for on-policy distill    | Every trajectory must pass through swegemma's real tools and limits                            |
| LoRA training (Path A/B, L9, L10) | Unsloth + PEFT                      | TRL (masking, DPO), axolotl, Liger, llm-compressor | Fits one RTX 5090; axolotl and llm-compressor de-risk Gemma 4 and the W4A16 train/serve gap    |
| Scaffold and phases (ADK YAML)    | adk-python source                   | mini-swe-agent, Agentless, auto-code-rover         | Know what the compiler does, then compare the minimal loop, fixed phases and structured search |
| Prompt and skill text             | SkillOpt                            | JetBrains benjamin, fable-method, DSPy/GEPA        | Optimize under held-out gating; inject budget rules into the instruction                       |
| Whitepaper evidence               | Agentless, AutoCodeRover, SWE-Swiss | Graft, FrontierHarness Eval, oh-my-pi              | Peer-reviewed designs first; repo-reported numbers as supporting claims                        |

---

## Appendix C - Near-Misses Considered and Excluded

| No. | Resources                                                                                                                                                                    | Why excluded                                                                                                                                       |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | QwenLM/Qwen3-Coder, deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Coder(-V2), MoonshotAI/Kimi-K2, TokenRhythm/NeoHorse                                                                                | Other base models. The single-model rule allows only gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct; at most a teacher, and Ornith-1 is the stronger open teacher.    |
| 2   | evalplus/evalplus, LiveCodeBench/LiveCodeBench, bigcode-project/bigcode-evaluation-harness                                                                                   | Function-level code benchmarks, not repository-level issue resolution (compendium finding 4).                                                      |
| 3   | multi-swe-bench/multi-swe-bench                                                                                                                                              | Multilingual issue resolution; the competition is Python-only.                                                                                     |
| 4   | ise-uiuc/magicoder, OpenCoder-llm/OpenCoder-llm, ByteDance-Seed/Seed-Coder, bigcode-project/starcoder2, InternLM/xtuner                                                      | Code-LLM pretraining or instruction-data recipes, or MoE-focused trainers; no path to a Gemma LoRA for agentic SWE.                                |
| 5   | Danau5tin/terminal-bench-rl, AMAP-ML/Tree-GRPO, WooooDyy/AgentGym-RL                                                                                                         | Agent RL codebases for terminal, web or QA tasks at small scale; superseded here by rLLM/SkyRL, and no license on terminal-bench-rl.               |
| 6   | colbymchenry/codegraph, DeusData/codebase-memory-mcp, abhigyanpatwari/GitNexus, jgravelle/jcodemunch-mcp, oraios/serena, Graphify-Labs/graphify, tirth8205/code-review-graph | Code-graph MCP servers. The sandbox is offline with fixed tools, and the harness already provides graph tools; Graft is kept as the evidence item. |
| 7   | rohitg00/agentmemory, vectorize-io/hindsight, supermemoryai/supermemory, topoteretes/cognee and 20+ memory layers                                                            | Cross-session memory. Each task runs in a fresh sandbox, and the BCF journal lives in /tmp within one session.                                     |
| 8   | Paritok-official/paritok-4b-v1, alexgreensh/token-optimizer, JuliusBrussee/caveman                                                                                           | Proxies or prose-style token savers for paid APIs; no middleware slot in the harness and no cost metric in scoring.                                |
| 9   | kvcache-ai/AgentENV, opensandbox-group/OpenSandbox, e2b-dev/E2B                                                                                                              | Sandbox infrastructure for large-scale RL; swegemma's Docker sandboxes already cover local rollouts.                                               |
| 10  | experientiallabs/world-model-optimizer                                                                                                                                       | Distillation runs through the hosted Tinker API; not free or equally accessible.                                                                   |
| 11  | QwenLM/Qwen-AgentWorld                                                                                                                                                       | Language world model for simulating environments; real executable SWE environments are available and cheaper to trust.                             |
| 12  | deepseek-ai/DeepSpec, NVIDIA/Model-Optimizer, lemonade/ollama/llama.cpp-class runtimes                                                                                       | Inference-speed or quantization tooling; the scoring server and model precision are fixed.                                                         |
| 13  | langfuse/langfuse                                                                                                                                                            | Heavy self-hosting (Postgres + ClickHouse + Redis + S3; compendium finding 7); MLflow is already in the plan.                                      |
| 14  | Badtheorylabs/interference-search                                                                                                                                            | Name overlaps BCF's 'fault interference', but it is a state-merging search for Countdown/MBPP. Do not cite it as fault-interference work.          |
| 15  | sunblaze-ucb/vero                                                                                                                                                            | Formal-verification (Lean 4) repository benchmark; unrelated to Python issue resolution.                                                           |
| 16  | asgeirtj/system_prompts_leaks, jujumilk3/leaked-system-prompts                                                                                                               | Leaked proprietary prompts; licensing and provenance risk for an open-source winner package.                                                       |
| 17  | Leonxlnx/unlazy, multica-ai/andrej-karpathy-skills, UiPath/coder_eval, AMAP-ML/LoopArena, aws-samples/sample-specship                                                        | Useful ideas already covered by higher-ranked items (acceptance ledgers, success criteria, agent eval suites, controller/worker loops).            |
| 18  | meta-pytorch/torchtune, huggingface/alignment-handbook, allenai/open-instruct, huggingface/open-r1, areal-project/AReaL                                                      | Listed as not-kept in the grokmuse LoRA report: unmaintained (torchtune) or not coding-agent specific.                                             |
| 19  | aorwall/moatless-tools, RUCAIBox/SWE-Master                                                                                                                                  | SWE scaffolds named in the report's not-kept list; covered by auto-code-rover, Agentless and mini-swe-agent.                                       |
